public static final int match(byte[] b, int ptr, byte[] src) { ... } In Java, you can have a method declared as public static final int match(...). The 'final' keyword on a method means it cannot be overridden. So it's a static final method returning int. In C#, static final is not a keyword; 'final' is not used. Instead, we can use 'static' and maybe 'sealed' for methods? In C#, you can mark a method as 'static' and 'sealed' if it's overriding a virtual method. But for a static method, you don't need 'sealed'. So we just translate to 'public static int Match(byte[] b, int ptr, byte[] src)'. Also, naming conventions: method names in C# are PascalCase. So 'match' becomes 'Match'. Also, the code inside: if (ptr + src.length > b.length) return -1; for (int i = 0; i < src.length; i++, ptr++) if (b[ptr] != src[i]) return -1; return ptr; In C#, array length is .Length. So src.Length, b.Length. Also, we need to use 'byte[]' same.
